- eval_entropy: 0.9635 (down from 1.014 — more confident)

**Training summary:**
- train_runtime: 688.4s (~11.5 min)


- train_loss: 0.0634
- 333 steps, 3 epochs

The eval loss ticked up slightly in epoch 3, which could signal early overfitting, but the accuracy continues climbing and entropy is dropping, suggesting the model is still learning meaningfully. The loss increase is marginal enough that performance should remain solid. Now I'm merging the adapter with the base model that's already loaded on CPU.

The trainer saves the final model state after epoch 3 rather than the best checkpoint, so that's what's getting merged even though epoch 2 had slightly lower eval loss. Given how tiny the difference is and that accuracy improved, this shouldn't be a problem in practice.

Now I'm looking at the full training metrics across all three epochs to see the overall trajectory.

The eval loss ticked up slightly in epoch 3 while accuracy kept improving—this makes sense because the model is getting more confident in its predictions, which can hurt calibration on wrong examples even as it gets more right overall. This is a normal trade-off and not a red flag. Let me wait for the merge to finish.

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The system continues operating as an independent scientific auditor (Reviewer role) for the OntoCoDec paper project (schema_semantic_constrained_ie). The primary ongoing task is monitoring and reviewing experiment results as they complete, with the current critical path being W5 LoRA fine-tuning and its post-training evaluation pipeline. The Reviewer's output channel is exclusively `reviewer_post_to_director`. All output must be in Chinese. The goal is to move the paper from Weak Reject (2.75/5) toward Accept (3.75-4.0/5).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **OntoCoDec**: Two-layer constrained decoding (grammar + semantic) for IE ontology compliance
   - **W5 LoRA fine-tuning**: Qwen3-8B + LoRA r=16 alpha=32 on SciERC SFT data (1762 train, 260 dev), 3 epochs, effective batch=16, lr=2e-4, cosine schedule, bf16
   - **W5 eval pipeline**: unconstrained fine-tuned eval → DR gate (>2%) → constrained fine-tuned eval → composability verification
   - **CF fallback trade-off (D020)**: CF↔DR re-routing, total violations +3.99pp with fallback disabled. Declared analytical finding.
   - **Grammar-first fallback**: When S(t) ∩ G(t) = ∅, semantic masking skipped. Rate: 36.6% on SciERC.
   - **Code revert**: type_mask_processor.py reverted from CF-modified (20,195 bytes) to original (15,666 bytes) before W5 eval
   - **GPU allocation**: cuda:0 and cuda:1 on westd-16980 (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell, 98GB each)
   - **Model merge**: LoRA adapter + base model → merged fp16 model via PeftModel.merge_and_unload()

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/scripts/train_lora_scierc.py`** (9,112 bytes)
     - W5 LoRA training script. TRL SFTTrainer, LoRA r=16, alpha=32, dropout=0.05
     - `merge_and_save(base_path, adapter_dir, merged_dir)` at line 83: loads base on CPU (bf16), loads adapter via PeftModel, merge_and_unload(), saves with safe_serialization=True
     - Post-training flow: train → save adapter → free GPU memory → merge_and_save → "All done."
     - Key defaults: model_path=/root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen3-8B-ms/Qwen/Qwen3-8B, output_dir=checkpoints/qwen3_8b_lora_scierc, merged_dir=checkpoints/qwen3_8b_lora_scierc_merged

4. Errors and Fixes:
